\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
such that

S\models\alpha
, it holds that

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\bigotimes^{n}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}^{p_{i}})
where
p_{i}\geq 1
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
is unsatisfiable,
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\chi)
for every
\chi\in K
so the result holds trivially.
In what follows, we assume that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
is satisfiable.

We start with case (ii), assuming that
\alpha
is of the form
C\sqsubseteq D
, with
C,D
basic concepts,
\mathbb{K}
is
\otimes
-idempotent and
\mathcal{O}
does not contain any GCI with
\top
as left-hand side.
Assume that
C
is satisfiable w.r.t.
\mathcal{O}
(otherwise

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(C\sqsubseteq D,\chi)
for every
\chi\in K
).
Let
\mathcal{I}_{C,S}
be the canonical model of
C
and
S
(we construct it in the same way as
\mathcal{I}_{C,S^{\mathbb{K}}}
except that we do not have the annotations).
Since
S\models\alpha
and
S
is a minimal set of axioms that entails
\alpha
,
by construction of
\mathcal{I}_{C,S}
,
there is a sequence
(\alpha_{1},\mathcal{I}^{1}_{C,S}),\ldots,(\alpha_{m},\mathcal{I}^{m}_{C,S})

of axioms and interpretations such that
d_{C}\in D^{\mathcal{I}^{m}_{C,S}}
, and
\mathcal{I}^{j}_{C,S}
is obtained from
\mathcal{I}^{j-1}_{C,S}
by applying the chase rule using
\alpha_{j}
.
By minimality of
S
all axioms in
S
occur in this sequence.
By construction of
\mathcal{I}_{C,S^{\mathbb{K}}}
, we have
an analogous sequence
((\alpha_{1},\kappa_{1}),\mathcal{I}^{1}_{C,S^{\mathbb{K}}}),\ldots,((\alpha_{m},\kappa_{m}),\mathcal{I}^{m}_{C,S^{\mathbb{K}}})
,
with the same axioms in
S
except that now they are annotated,
and we have
(d_{C},\kappa)\in D^{\mathcal{I}^{m}_{C,S^{\mathbb{K}}}}
with

\kappa=\bigotimes^{m}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}
.
Since
\mathbb{K}
is
\otimes
-idempotent,
we have that

\kappa=\bigotimes^{n}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}
.
By Theorem
4.7
, since
\mathbb{K}
is
\otimes
-idempotent, and
\mathcal{O}
(hence
S
) does not contain any GCI with
\top
as left-hand side
S^{\mathbb{K}}\models(C\sqsubseteq D,\kappa)
.
Since
S^{\mathbb{K}}
is a subset of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
,
by the semantics of
\mathbb{K}
-annotated
{\cal E\!LHI}^{n}_{\bot}
,
we have that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models S^{\mathbb{K}}\models(C\sqsubseteq D,\kappa)
, which means that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(C\sqsubseteq D,\kappa)
.

We now consider case (i):
\alpha
is an assertion, a BCQ, or an RI and
\mathbb{K}
may not be
\otimes
-idempotent
.
If
\alpha
is a BCQ (or an assertion), let
\mathcal{I}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}
be the canonical model of
S^{\mathbb{K}}
. As in the GCI case, we can obtain a sequence
((\alpha_{1},\kappa_{1}),\mathcal{I}^{1}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}),\ldots,((\alpha_{m},\kappa_{m}),\mathcal{I}^{m}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}})
where
\mathcal{I}^{j}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}
is obtained from
\mathcal{I}^{j-1}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}
by applying the chase rule using
(\alpha_{j},\kappa_{j})
and
\mathcal{I}^{m}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
with
\kappa=\bigotimes^{m}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}=\bigotimes^{n}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}^{p_{i}}
where each
p_{i}\geq 1
is the number of times
(\alpha_{i},\kappa_{i})
occurs in the sequence
((\alpha_{1},\kappa_{1}),\mathcal{I}^{1}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}}),\ldots,((\alpha_{m},\kappa_{m}),\mathcal{I}^{m}_{S^{\mathbb{K}}})
.
Then by Theorem
4.5
, we obtain
S^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.
Finally, if
\alpha
is an RI of the form
P\sqsubseteq Q
, we proceed in the same way, using the canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{P,S}
of
P
and
S
and Theorem
4.9
. ∎

See
5.1

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{O}\not\models\alpha
, i.e. there is a model
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{O}
such that
\mathcal{I}\not\models\alpha
. By Claim (
i
) of Lemma
3.5
, there exists a model
\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{K}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
that coincides with
\mathcal{I}
on its non-annotated part. There is no
\kappa\in K
such that
\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
(otherwise we would contradict the fact that
\mathcal{I}\not\models\alpha
). It follows that there is no
\kappa\in K
such that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
, thus
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\mathbb{0}
.

Assume that
\mathbb{K}
is positive,
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\mathbb{0}
and either (i)
\alpha
is an assertion, a BCQ, or an RI or (ii)
\mathbb{K}
is
\otimes
-idempotent and
\mathcal{O}
does not contain any GCI with
\top
as left-hand side.
Since
\mathbb{K}
is positive and complete (or
\omega
-complete with
K
countable),
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\mathbb{0}
means that (
\dagger
) there is no
\kappa\in K
such that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
and
\kappa\neq\mathbb{0}
. Indeed, by (
\omega
-)completeness, for every
\kappa_{0}
such that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa_{0})
, it holds that
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)}\kappa=\kappa_{0}\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\kappa),\kappa\neq\kappa_{0}}\kappa
, so by positivity,
\kappa_{0}=\mathbb{0}
.
Assume for a contradiction that
\mathcal{O}\models\alpha
.
Then, there is a minimal subset
S^{\mathbb{K}}=\{(\alpha_{1},\kappa_{1}),\ldots,(\alpha_{n},\kappa_{n})\}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
such that

S\models\alpha
.
By Lemma
C.1
,

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(\alpha,\bigotimes^{n}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}^{p_{i}})
with all
p_{i}
’s greater or equal to
1
.
Since
\mathbb{K}
is positive and every
\kappa_{i}
is different from
\mathbb{0}
by definition of annotated ontologies, then
\bigotimes^{n}_{i=1}\kappa_{i}^{p_{i}}\neq\mathbb{0}
,
which contradicts (
\dagger
).
∎

See
5.4

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
be the canonical model of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
(note that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
is satisfiable since it contains only assertions).
By Theorem
4.5
, for every
\kappa\in K
, we have

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(q,\kappa)
iff
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(q,\kappa)
.
Hence
\mathcal{P}(q,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}\models(q,\kappa)}\kappa=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(q,\kappa)}\kappa
.

By definition,
\mathcal{P}_{DB}(q,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\bigoplus_{\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})}\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t})\in q}\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))
, where
\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})
is the set of all matches of
q
in
\mathcal{O}
.
We prove that

\{\kappa\mid\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(q,\kappa)\}=\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t})\in q}\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))\mid\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})\}.

The result follows immediately from this and the idempotency of
\oplus
.

Since
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
contains only assertions, by construction of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
, for every
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
it holds that
A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(a,\lambda(A(a)))\mid A(a)\in\mathcal{O}\}
and for every
R\in{\sf N_{R}}
it holds that
R^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(a,b,\lambda(R(a,b)))\mid R(a,b)\in\mathcal{O}\}
. Let
\boldsymbol{q}
be the extended version of
q
.

[“
\subseteq
”]
Let
\kappa
be s.t.
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(q,\kappa)
. There is a match

\pi
of
\boldsymbol{q}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
s.t.
\kappa=\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)
. It is easy to see that
\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})
, and that for every
P(\vec{t})\in q
with
P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}
,
\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))=\pi(t)
.
Hence
\kappa\in\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t})\in q}\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))\mid\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})\}
.

[“
\supseteq
”]
Given a match
\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})
, the function
\pi^{\prime}
which for every
P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}

maps
\vec{t}
to
\pi(\vec{t})
and
t
to
\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))
is a match of
\boldsymbol{q}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
. Thus, for every match
\pi\in\Pi(q,\mathcal{O})
, it holds that
\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t})\in q}\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))\in\{\kappa\mid\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(q,\kappa)\}
.
∎

See
5.6

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}}=\langle\mathcal{D},\lambda\rangle
be the annotated database corresponding to the assertions in
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
and let
\{C_{i}\sqsubseteq A\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
be the set of all GCIs in
\mathcal{O}
and
\Pi(q_{C_{i}},\mathcal{D})
be the set of all matches of the query
q_{C_{i}}(a)
in
\mathcal{D}
;
1\leq i\leq n
.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
be the canonical model of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
(note that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
is satisfiable since it does not feature
\bot
).
Since
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
contains only assertions and GCIs of the form
C_{i}\sqsubseteq A
labelled with
\mathbb{1}
where
C_{i}
does not contain
A
, by construction of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}
, it holds that

•

B^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(b,\lambda(B(b)))\mid B(b)\in\mathcal{D}\}
, for every
B\in{\sf N_{C}}\setminus\{A\}
;

•

R^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(b,c,\lambda(R(b,c)))\mid R(b,c)\in\mathcal{D}\}
, for every
R\in{\sf N_{R}}
; and

•

A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(b,\lambda(A(b)))\mid A(b)\in\mathcal{D}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}C_{i}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}
.

By Lemma
3.10
,

\{\kappa\mid(d,\kappa)\in{C_{i}}^{\mathcal{I}}\}=\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(x)}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(x)),\pi(x)=d\}
, where
\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(x)
is the extended version of
{q_{C_{i}}}(x)
(
1\leq i\leq n
).
Hence,

A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}=\{(b,\lambda(A(b)))\mid A(b)\in\mathcal{D}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\{(b,\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(b)}\pi(t))\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(b))\}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\{\kappa\mid\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(A(a),\kappa)\}=

\displaystyle\{\lambda(A(a))\mid A(a)\in\mathcal{D}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(a)}\pi(t))\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C_{i}}}(a))\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\lambda(\pi(A(a)))\mid\pi\in\Pi(A(a),\mathcal{D})\right\}\cup

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t})\in q_{C_{i}}(a)}\lambda(\pi(P(\vec{t})))\mid\pi\in\Pi(q_{C_{i}}(a),\mathcal{D})\right\}.

As in the proof of Theorem
5.4
,
\mathcal{P}(A(a),\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}\models(A(a),\kappa)}\kappa
(by Theorem
4.5
) and the result follows immediately from the equality above and idempotency of
\oplus
.
∎

See
5.11

Proof.

We denote by
\boldsymbol{q}
the extended version of
q
. We show that

\bigcap_{\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})=\bigcap_{(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})}\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal}),

so that

\mathcal{P}(q,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}})=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in\bigcap_{\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})}\kappa=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in\bigcap_{(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})}\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})}\kappa=\mathcal{P}_{Dat}^{\texttt{SAM}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}},\mathsf{goal}).

[“
\supseteq
”]
For every
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
, we define

f(\mathcal{I})=(I,\mu^{I})
where

\displaystyle I={}

\displaystyle\{A(a)\mid\exists\kappa,\mathcal{I}\models(A(a),\kappa)\}\cup\{R(a,b)\mid\exists\kappa,\mathcal{I}\models(R(a,b),\kappa)\}\cup{}

\displaystyle\{F_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\text{ GCI or RI of }\mathcal{O}\}\cup\{\mathsf{goal}\mid\mathcal{I}\models\boldsymbol{q}\},

and

\mu^{I}(\alpha)={\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\alpha})
if
\alpha
is an assertion,

\mu^{I}(F_{\alpha})=\{\lambda(\alpha)\}
if
\alpha
is a GCI or an RI, and

\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})={\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})=\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q})\}
.
We show that
f(\mathcal{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})
.

1.

Since
\mathcal{I}
satisfies every annotated assertion of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
, it is easy to check that
\mathcal{D}\subseteq I
, that for every
\alpha\in\mathcal{D}\cap\mathcal{O}
,
\lambda^{\prime}(\alpha)=\lambda(\alpha)
belongs to
\mu^{I}(\alpha)={\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\alpha})
and that for every
F_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{D}\setminus\mathcal{O}
,
\lambda^{\prime}(F_{\alpha})=\lambda(\alpha)
belongs to
\mu^{I}(F_{\alpha})=\{\lambda(\alpha)\}
.

2.

Let
\phi(\vec{x},\vec{y})\rightarrow H(\vec{x})\in\Sigma
be such that there is a homomorphism
h:\phi(\vec{x},\vec{y})\mapsto I
.
We consider only the case where
\phi(\vec{x},\vec{y})\rightarrow H(\vec{x})
corresponds to an annotated GCI
(C\sqsubseteq A,\kappa^{\prime})\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
; the case of role inclusions is analogous:
\phi(\vec{x},\vec{y})\rightarrow H(\vec{x})
is of the form
q_{C}(x)\wedge F_{C\sqsubseteq A}\rightarrow A(x)
.
Assume that
h(q_{C}(x))=\beta_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\beta_{n}
.
By construction of
I
and
\mu^{I}
, for every
1\leq i\leq n
, since
\beta_{i}\in I
,
\mu^{I}(\beta_{i})\neq\emptyset
and for every
\kappa_{i}\in\mu^{I}(\beta_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}\models(\beta_{i},\kappa_{i})
.
By Lemma
3.10
, we have

\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)),\pi(x)=h(x)^{\mathcal{I}}\}=\{\kappa\mid(h(x)^{\mathcal{I}},\kappa)\in C^{\mathcal{I}}\},

where
\boldsymbol{q_{C}}
is the extended version of
q_{C}
.
For every
(\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n})\in\mu^{I}(\beta_{1})\times\dots\times\mu^{I}(\beta_{n})
, there exists

\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x))
such that
\pi
maps
\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)
to
\{(\beta_{1},\kappa_{1}),\dots,(\beta_{n},\kappa_{n})\}
,
so

(h(x)^{\mathcal{I}},\kappa_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\kappa_{n})\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
.
As
\mathcal{I}\models(C\sqsubseteq A,\kappa^{\prime})
, we get
(h(x)^{\mathcal{I}},\kappa_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\kappa_{n}\otimes\kappa^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
. It then follows, since
\mu^{I}(F_{C\sqsubseteq A})=\{\kappa^{\prime}\}
by definition of
\mu^{I}
, that

h(A(x))\in I
and

\{\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n+1}\kappa_{i}\mid(\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n},\kappa_{n+1})\in\mu^{I}(\beta_{1})\times\dots\times\mu^{I}(\beta_{n})\times\mu^{I}(F_{C\sqsubseteq A})\}\subseteq\mu^{I}(h(A(x)))
.

Hence,

\bigcap_{\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})\ =\bigcap_{\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}},f(\mathcal{I})=(I,\mu^{I})}\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})\ \supseteq\ \bigcap_{(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})}\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal}).

[“
\subseteq
”]
For every
(I,\mu^{I})
s.t.
(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})
, we define

g(I,\mu^{I})=(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},K,\cdot^{\mathcal{I}})
by setting
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}={\sf N_{I}}
,
a^{\mathcal{I}}=a
for every
a\in{\sf N_{I}}
,

A^{\mathcal{I}}=\{(a,\kappa)\mid A(a)\in I,\kappa\in\mu^{I}(A(a))\}
for
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
, and

R^{\mathcal{I}}=\{(a,b,\kappa)\mid R(a,b)\in I,\kappa\in\mu^{I}(R(a,b))\}
for
R\in{\sf N_{R}}
.
We show that
g(I,\mu^{I})\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
and that
{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})\subseteq\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})
.

•

Since
\mathcal{D}\subseteq I
and for every
\alpha\in\mathcal{D}
,
\lambda^{\prime}(\alpha)\in\mu^{I}(\alpha)
, since
\lambda^{\prime}(\alpha)=\lambda(\alpha)
for every assertion of
\mathcal{O}
, it is easy to check that
\mathcal{I}
satisfies every annotated assertion of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
.

•

Let
(C\sqsubseteq A,\kappa^{\prime})
be an annotated GCI of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}
(the case of RIs is similar).
Let
(e,\kappa)\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
and
\boldsymbol{q_{C}}
be the extended version of
q_{C}
.
By Lemma
3.10
, we get that

\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)),\pi(x)=e\}=\{\kappa\mid(e,\kappa)\in C^{\mathcal{I}}\}
,
so there exist some
\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x))
such that
\pi(x)=e
and
\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)}\pi(t)=\kappa
.
Let
(\beta_{1},\kappa_{1}),\dots,(\beta_{n},\kappa_{n})
be the image of
\boldsymbol{q_{C}}(x)
by
\pi
and
h
be the restriction of
\pi
to the non-annotated part of
\mathcal{I}
.
By construction,
\kappa=\kappa_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\kappa_{n}
and
for
1\leq i\leq n
,
\beta_{i}\in I
,
\kappa_{i}\in\mu^{I}(\beta_{i})
, and
h
is a homomorphism from
q_{C}
to
I
such that
h(q_{C}(x))=\beta_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\beta_{n}
and
h(x)=e
.

Since
q_{C}(x)\wedge F_{C\sqsubseteq A}\rightarrow A(x)
is in
\Sigma
and
h
is a homomorphism from
q_{C}(x)\wedge F_{C\sqsubseteq A}
to
I
, since
(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})
, it follows that
A(e)\in I
and
\kappa_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\kappa_{n}\otimes\kappa^{\prime}
is in
\mu^{I}(A(e))
, i.e.
(e,\kappa\otimes\kappa^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
. Hence
\mathcal{I}\models(C\sqsubseteq A,\kappa^{\prime})
.

•

Let
\kappa\in{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})=\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{q})\}
. There is a mapping
\pi
from
\boldsymbol{q}
to
\mathcal{I}
such that
\kappa=\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)
. By construction of
\mathcal{I}
, this means that for each
P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}
,
\pi(P(\vec{t}))\in I
and
\pi(t)\in\mu^{I}(\pi(P(\vec{t})))
.
Since
q\rightarrow\mathsf{goal}
is in
\Sigma
and
\pi
is a homomorphism from
q
to
I
, since
(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})
, it follows that
\mathsf{goal}\in I
and
\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)\in\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})
.
Hence

\kappa\in\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})
and
{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})\subseteq\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal})
.

\text{Hence }\bigcap_{\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}}}{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})\ \subseteq\bigcap_{(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}}),g(I,\mu^{I})=\mathcal{I}}{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}}({\boldsymbol{q}})\ \subseteq\bigcap_{(I,\mu^{I})\models(\Sigma,\mathcal{D}^{\mathbb{K}})}\mu^{I}(\mathsf{goal}).\qed

See
5.12

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a satisfiable

{\cal E\!LHI}^{n}_{\bot}
ontology with annotated versions
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\langle\mathcal{O},\lambda\rangle
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}=\langle\mathcal{O},h\circ\lambda\rangle
,
where

\mathbb{K}_{1}=(K_{1},\oplus,\otimes,\mathbb{0},\mathbb{1})
and
\mathbb{K}_{2}=(K_{2},+,\cdot,0,1)

are commutative additively idempotent
\omega
-complete semirings.

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\{(\alpha,\lambda(\alpha))\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{O}\}\text{ and }\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}=\{(\alpha,h(\lambda(\alpha)))\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{O}\}.

Recall that since
\mathcal{O}
is satisfiable, by Lemma
3.5
,
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}
are satisfiable as well.
Moreover, since
h
is
a semiring homomorphism,

h(\mathbb{0})=0
,
h(\mathbb{1})=1
, and for all
a,b\in K_{1}
,
h(a\oplus b)=h(a)+h(b)
and
h(a\otimes b)=h(a)\cdot h(b)
,
and since this homomorphism is
\omega
-complete,
h(\bigoplus_{i\in I}a_{i})=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{412879}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{294913}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}}\displaylimits_{i\in I}h(a_{i})
for every countable index set
I
. We prove the theorem using two claims.

Claim C.2
.

Let
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\langle\mathcal{O},\lambda\rangle
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}=\langle\mathcal{O},h\circ\lambda\rangle

be as in this proof.

•

For every assertion or BCQ
\alpha
, and every
\kappa\in K_{1}
, if

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
then

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,h(\kappa))
.

•

For every RI
\alpha
whose left-hand side is satisfiable w.r.t.
\mathcal{O}
, and every
\kappa\in K_{1}
, if

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
then

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,h(\kappa))
.

•

If
\mathbb{K}_{1}
and
\mathbb{K}_{2}
are also multiplicatively idempotent and
\mathcal{O}
does not contain GCI with
\top
as left-hand side, then, for every GCI
\alpha
between basic concepts whose left-hand side is satisfiable w.r.t.
\mathcal{O}
, for every
\kappa\in K_{1}
, if

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
then

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,h(\kappa))
.

Proof of the claim.

First assume
\alpha
is an assertion or BCQ.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}

and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}
be the canonical models of

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}
, respectively (see Section
4.2
for the definition). By Theorem
4.5
, if

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
then

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.
By definition of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}
and the fact that
h
is a semiring homomorphism, so that
h(a)\cdot h(b)=h(a\otimes b)
, the canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}

has the same construction as
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}
, except
for the annotations: one can show by induction that if
(\vec{e},\chi)\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}}
for some concept or role name
E
, then
(\vec{e},h(\chi))\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}\models(\alpha,h(\kappa))

and, by Theorem
4.5
,

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,h(\kappa))
.

The proofs for the cases where
\alpha
is an RI of the form
P\sqsubseteq Q
or a GCI of the form
C\sqsubseteq D
and fulfills the conditions stated in the claim are similar to the case where
\alpha
is a BCQ, except that we use the canonical models
\mathcal{I}_{P,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{i}}}
of
P
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{i}}
or
\mathcal{I}_{C,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{i}}}
of
C
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{i}}
respectively (see Section
4.2
for the definition) and Theorems
4.9
and
4.7
respectively. Note that the conditions of the claim are the same as the conditions to apply these theorems. ∎

Claim C.3
.

Let
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\langle\mathcal{O},\lambda\rangle
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}=\langle\mathcal{O},h\circ\lambda\rangle

be as in this proof.

•

For every assertion or BCQ
\alpha
, for every
\kappa^{\prime}\in K_{2}
, if
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})
then there exists
\kappa\in K_{1}
such that
h(\kappa)=\kappa^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.

•

For every RI
\alpha
whose left-hand side is satisfiable w.r.t.
\mathcal{O}
, for every
\kappa^{\prime}\in K_{2}
, if
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})
then there exists
\kappa\in K_{1}
such that
h(\kappa)=\kappa^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.

•

If
\mathbb{K}_{1}
and
\mathbb{K}_{2}
are also multiplicatively idempotent and
\mathcal{O}
does not contain GCI with
\top
as left-hand side, then, for every GCI
\alpha
between basic concepts whose left-hand side is satisfiable w.r.t.
\mathcal{O}
, for every
\kappa^{\prime}\in K_{2}
, if
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})
then there exists
\kappa\in K_{1}
such that
h(\kappa)=\kappa^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.

Proof of the claim.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}

and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}
be the canonical models of

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}
, respectively.
Assume
\alpha
is an assertion or a BCQ.
By Theorem
4.5
, if

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})
then

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})
.
By definition of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}
and the fact that
h
is a semiring homomorphism, the canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}

has the same construction as
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}
, except
for the annotations: one can show by induction that if
(\vec{e},\chi^{\prime})\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}}}
for some concept or role name
E
, then there exists some
(\vec{e},\chi)\in E^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}}
such that
\chi^{\prime}=h(\chi)
.
It follows that there exists
\kappa\in K_{1}
such that
\kappa^{\prime}=h(\kappa)
and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)

and, by Theorem
4.5
,

\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)
.

The proofs for the cases where
\alpha
is an RI of the form
P\sqsubseteq Q
or a GCI of the form
C\sqsubseteq D
and fulfills the conditions stated in the claim are similar to the case where
\alpha
is a BCQ, but use Theorems
4.9
and
4.7
respectively (as we did in the proof of Claim
C.2
).
∎

By Claims
C.2
and
C.3
, the definitions of
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
,
\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}})
,
\omega
-completeness of
h
and additive idempotency,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}})=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{412879}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{294913}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}}\displaylimits_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{2}}\models(\alpha,\kappa^{\prime})}\kappa^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{589827}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{412879}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{294913}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\vphantom{\sum}}}\displaylimits_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)}h(\kappa)=h(\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\models(\alpha,\kappa)}\kappa)=h(\mathcal{P}(\alpha,\mathcal{O}^{\mathbb{K}_{1}})).\qed

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
6

Recall that in this section, we consider the case where a
\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
-annotated ontology
\mathcal{O}
is annotated by a function
\lambda_{\sf X}:\mathcal{O}\mapsto{\sf X}\cup\{1\}
.
We will often use the canonical model of the ontology. For convenience, we recall in Figure
3
its construction in more details for the case where
\mathcal{O}
is a satisfiable
\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
-annotated ontology in
normal form
.

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{I}_{i}
where

\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}={\sf N_{I}}
,
a^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}=a
for every
a\in{\sf N_{I}}
,
A^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}=\{(a,n)\mid(A(a),n)\in\mathcal{O}\}
for every
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
and
R^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}=\{(a,b,n)\mid(R(a,b),n)\in\mathcal{O}\}
for every
R\in{\sf N_{R}}
, and
\mathcal{I}_{i+1}
results from applying the chase rule to
\mathcal{I}_{i}
so that we are in one of the following cases.

\mathsf{R}_{1}

(R\sqsubseteq S,n)\in\mathcal{O}
or
(R^{-}\sqsubseteq S^{-},n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,e,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
S^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=S^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(d,e,{o\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{2}

(R\sqsubseteq S^{-},n)\in\mathcal{O}
or
(R^{-}\sqsubseteq S,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,e,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
S^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=S^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(e,d,{o\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{3}

(A\sqsubseteq B,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,o)\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
B^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=B^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(d,{o\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{4}

(A_{1}\sqcap A_{2}\sqsubseteq B,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,o_{1})\in A_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
(d,o_{2})\in A_{2}^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
B^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=B^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(d,{o_{1}\times o_{2}\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{5}

(\exists R.A\sqsubseteq B,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,e,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
(e,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
B^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=B^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(d,{o\times o^{\prime}\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{6}

(\exists R^{-}.A\sqsubseteq B,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,e,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
(d,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
B^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=B^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(e,{o\times o^{\prime}\times n})\}
.

\mathsf{R}_{7}

(A\sqsubseteq\exists R,n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,o)\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{x\}
and
R^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(d,x,{o\times n})\}
where
x\notin\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
is a fresh domain element.

\mathsf{R}_{8}

(A\sqsubseteq\exists R^{-},n)\in\mathcal{O}
,
(d,o)\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{x\}
and
R^{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}=R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\cup\{(x,d,{o\times n})\}
where
x\notin\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
is a fresh domain element.

Figure 3:
Construction of the canonical model of a satisfiable
\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
-annotated
{\cal E\!LHI}^{n}_{\bot}
ontology in normal form.

A,A_{1},A_{2}\in{\sf N_{C}}\cup\{\top\}
,
B\in{\sf N_{C}}
, and
R,S\in{\sf N_{R}}
(recall that RIs and GCIs of
\mathcal{O}
are of these forms, except negative RIs and GCIs with
\bot
as right-hand side, which are not needed since
\mathcal{O}
is satisfiable). Since we assume that axioms of
\mathcal{O}
are annotated with variables or
1
, all annotations are monomials.

See
6.1

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}
be the canonical model of
\mathcal{O}
.
Since every axiom of
\mathcal{O}
is annotated with some variable from
{\sf X}
or
1
, it follows from the construction of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}
that for every
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
,
(a,m)\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
implies that
m
is a product of variables from
{\sf X}
, i.e. a monomial over
{\sf X}
, and similarly for roles.
If
\alpha
is an assertion
P(\vec{a})
, then by Theorem
4.5
,
\mathcal{O}\models(\alpha,m)
implies
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}\models(P(\vec{a}),m)
, i.e.
(\vec{a},m)\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
, so
m
is a monomial.
If
\alpha
is a BCQ
q
whose extended version is
\boldsymbol{q}
, then by Theorem
4.5
,
\mathcal{O}\models(\alpha,m)
implies
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}\models(q,m)
, i.e.
m\in{\sf prov}_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}({\boldsymbol{q}})=\{\bigotimes_{P(\vec{t},t)\in\boldsymbol{q}}\pi(t)\mid\pi\in\nu_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}(\boldsymbol{q})\}
where
\pi(t)
is the last element of the tuple
\pi(\vec{t},t)\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
. Hence, since a product of monomials is a monomial,
m
is a monomial.
∎

D.1
Annotated Assertion Entailment from
{\cal E\!LHI}^{n}_{\bot}
Ontologies

Proof of Theorem
6.4

Before proving the theorem, we first establish the following technical lemmas.
Recall that
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
is obtained by starting with

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle\cup\{(\top(a),1)\mid a\in\mathsf{ind}(\mathcal{O})\cup\{a_{\top}\}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(A\sqsubseteq A,1)\mid A\in({\sf N_{C}}\cap\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{O}))\cup\{\top,\bot\}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(R\sqsubseteq R,1),(R^{-}\sqsubseteq R^{-},1),(\exists R.\bot\sqsubseteq\bot,1),(\exists R^{-}.\bot\sqsubseteq\bot,1)\mid R\in{\sf N_{R}}\cap\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{O})\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),v)\mid(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},v)\in\mathcal{O}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(P_{2})\sqsubseteq\bot,v)\mid(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,v)\in\mathcal{O}\},

and extending
\mathcal{S}
through an iterative application of the rules from Table
3
until no more rules are applicable.
For point (1) of Theorem
6.4
, i.e. soundness of the completion algorithm, we will use the following lemma.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
be the result of saturating
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{I}
be a model of
\mathcal{O}
. For every assertion, GCI, or RI
\alpha
and every monomial
m
, if
(\alpha,{m})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
it holds that:

1.

if
\alpha
is an assertion or an RI (positive or negative), then
\mathcal{I}\models(\alpha,{m})
,

2.

if
\alpha
is a GCI of the form
C\sqsubseteq D
, then for every
monomial

n
and domain element
e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
,
(e,n)\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
implies
(e,n\times m)\in D^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{0}=\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}_{i+1}
be obtained by applying a completion rule to
\mathcal{S}_{i}
, so that
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})=\mathcal{S}_{n}
for some
n\geq 0
.
We show by induction on
i
that for every assertion, GCI, or RI
\alpha
and every monomial
m
, if
(\alpha,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
it holds that:

1.

if
\alpha
is an assertion or an RI (positive or negative), then
\mathcal{I}\models(\alpha,m)
,

2.

if
\alpha
is a GCI of the form
C\sqsubseteq D
, then for every
monomial

n
and domain element
e\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
,
(e,n)\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
implies
(e,n\times m)\in D^{\mathcal{I}}
.

For the base case
i=0
, we can actually show that
(\alpha,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{0}=\mathcal{S}
implies
\mathcal{I}\models(\alpha,m)
, which directly implies points (1-2). Indeed, we have the following cases.

•

If
(\alpha,m)\in\mathcal{O}
, since
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{I}\models(\alpha,m)
.

•

If
(\alpha,{m})
is of one of the forms:
(\top(a),1)
for some
a\in\mathsf{ind}(\mathcal{O})\cup\{a_{\top}\}
,
(A\sqsubseteq A,1)
for some
A\in({\sf N_{C}}\cap\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{O}))\cup\{\top,\bot\}
,
(R\sqsubseteq R,1)
,
(R^{-}\sqsubseteq R^{-},1)
,
(\exists R.\bot\sqsubseteq\bot,1)
or
(\exists R^{-}.\bot\sqsubseteq\bot,1)
for some
R\in{\sf N_{R}}\cap\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{O})
,
\mathcal{I}\models(\alpha,m)
trivially (recall that
\bot^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
and
(\exists R^{(-)}.\bot)^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
).

•

If
(\alpha,{m})
is of the form
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)
for some
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathcal{O}
,
since
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)
. It follows directly from the definition of the annotated interpretation of
\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})
and
\mathsf{inv}(P_{2})
that
\mathcal{I}\models(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)
.

•

If
(\alpha,{m})
is of the form
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(P_{2})\sqsubseteq\bot,m)
for some
(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,m)\in\mathcal{O}
, since
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathcal{O}
,
(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2})^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
and so
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}))^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
. Hence
\mathcal{I}\models(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(P_{2})\sqsubseteq\bot,m)

Assume that the property is true for some
i
and consider
(\alpha,{m})\in\mathcal{S}_{i+1}
. If
(\alpha,{m})\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
, we obtain the result by induction hypothesis. Otherwise,
(\alpha,{m})
has been added by the rule applied to obtain
\mathcal{S}_{i+1}
from
\mathcal{S}_{i}
and we have 11 possible cases depending on which completion rule has been applied.

\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{0}

(\alpha,{m})=(A\sqsubseteq\bot,{m_{0}\times m_{1}\times m_{2}\times m_{3}})
and the induction hypothesis applies to the axioms
(A\sqsubseteq\exists P,m_{0})
,
(P\sqsubseteq P_{1},m_{1})
,
(P\sqsubseteq P_{2},m_{2})
and
(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
.
Moreover, by the form of the axioms that can be added by the saturation rules,
(A\sqsubseteq\exists P,m_{0})
is actually in
\mathcal{O}
so satisfied by
\mathcal{I}
, and either
(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
is in
\mathcal{O}
, or
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(P_{2})\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
is in
\mathcal{O}
, and in both cases,
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
. Thus

\mathcal{I}\models(A\sqsubseteq\exists P,m_{0})
,
\mathcal{I}\models(P\sqsubseteq P_{1},m_{1})
,
\mathcal{I}\models(P\sqsubseteq P_{2},m_{2})
and
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2}\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
.
It follows that
A^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
: otherwise, if there was
c\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and
n\in\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
such that
(c,n)\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
, there would be
(c,d,n\times m_{0})\in P^{\mathcal{I}}
, so
(c,d,n\times m_{0}\times m_{1})\in P_{1}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(c,d,n\times m_{0}\times m_{2})\in P_{2}^{\mathcal{I}}
, hence
(c,d,n\times n\times m_{0}\times m_{0}\times m_{1}\times m_{2})\in(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2})^{\mathcal{I}}
, contradicting
(P_{1}\sqcap P_{2})^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
.
Hence
\mathcal{I}\models(A\sqsubseteq\bot,m)
for every
m
, in particular for
m={m_{0}\times m_{1}\times m_{2}\times m_{3}}
, and the condition required for the GCI
\alpha
follows.

\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}

(\alpha,{m})=(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{3},{m_{1}\times m_{2}})
and the induction hypothesis applies to
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m_{1})
and
(P_{2}\sqsubseteq P_{3},m_{2})
, so that
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m_{1})
and
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{2}\sqsubseteq P_{3},m_{2})
.
For all
c,d\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and
n\in\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
, if
(c,d,n)\in P_{1}^{\mathcal{I}}
, then
(c,d,n\times m_{1})\in P_{2}^{\mathcal{I}}
which means that
(c,d,n\times m_{1}\times m_{2})\in P_{3}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Hence
\mathcal{I}\models(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{3},{m_{1}\times m_{2}})
, as required for the RI
\alpha
.

\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{2}

(\alpha,{m})=(M\sqcap N\sqsubseteq C,{m_{1}\times m_{2}})
and the induction hypothesis applies to
(M\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})
and
(A\sqcap N\sqsubseteq C,m_{2})
.
Let
c\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and
n
be a monomial. If
(c,n)\in(M\sqcap N)^{\mathcal{I}}
, there exists
(c,n_{1})\in M^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(c,n_{2})\in N^{\mathcal{I}}
such that
n_{1}\times n_{2}=n
. Moreover,
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
must be monomials.
By induction hypothesis, it follows that
(c,n_{1}\times m_{1})\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
, so
(c,n_{1}\times m_{1}\times n_{2})\in(A\sqcap N)^{\mathcal{I}}
. By induction hypothesis, since
n_{1}\times m_{1}\times n_{2}
is a monomial, we obtain
(c,n_{1}\times m_{1}\times n_{2}\times m_{2})\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
, i.e.
(c,n\times m_{1}\times m_{2})\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
, as required for the GCI
\alpha
.

\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{3}

(\alpha,{m})=(A\sqcap A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}\sqsubseteq D,m\times n\times o\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})
and the induction hypothesis applies to

•

(A\sqsubseteq\exists Q,m_{0})
, which actually belongs to
\mathcal{O}
by the form of the axioms that can be added by the saturation rules, so is satisfied by
\mathcal{I}
,

•

(Q\sqsubseteq P,m)
,

•

(Q\sqsubseteq P_{i},m_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,
k\geq 0
,

•

(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i},n_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,
k\geq 0
, which actually belong to
\mathcal{O}
by the form of the axioms that can be added by the saturation rules, so are satisfied by
\mathcal{I}
,

•

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i},o_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}
,
k^{\prime}\geq 0
,

•

(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq C,n),
and

•

(\exists P.C\sqsubseteq D,o)
, which actually belongs to
\mathcal{O}
by the form of the axioms that can be added by the saturation rules, so is satisfied by
\mathcal{I}
.

Let
c\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and
r
be a monomial.
Assume that
(c,r)\in(A\sqcap A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k})^{\mathcal{I}}
.
There exists
(c,r_{0})\in A^{\mathcal{I}}
, and
(c,r_{i})\in A_{i}^{\mathcal{I}}
for
1\leq i\leq k
such that
r=r_{0}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}
. Note that all
r_{j}
in this product must be monomials since
r
is a monomial.

Since
\mathcal{I}\models(A\sqsubseteq\exists Q,m_{0})
, there exists
(c,d,r_{0}\times m_{0})\in Q^{\mathcal{I}}
.

For every
1\leq i\leq k
, since
\mathcal{I}\models(Q\sqsubseteq P_{i},m_{i})
,
(c,d,r_{0}\times m_{0}\times m_{i})\in P_{i}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Hence, since
(c,r_{i})\in A_{i}^{\mathcal{I}}
,
it follows that
(d,r_{0}\times m_{0}\times m_{i}\times r_{i})\in(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i})^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Since
\mathcal{I}\models(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i},n_{i})
,
then
(d,r_{0}\times m_{0}\times m_{i}\times r_{i}\times n_{i})\in B_{i}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

For every
1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}
, since
\top^{\mathcal{I}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\{1\}
and
1
is a monomial, by induction hypothesis on
(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i},o_{i})
,
then
(d,o_{i})\in{B^{\prime}_{i}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Since the product of a monomial by itself is the same monomial in the
\sf{Why}[{\sf X}]
semiring, it follows that
(d,r_{0}\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times r_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})\in(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})^{\mathcal{I}}
, i.e.
(d,r\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})\in(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Hence, by induction hypothesis on
(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq C,n)
, it follows that

(d,r\times m_{0}\times n\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})\in C^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Moreover, since
\mathcal{I}\models(Q\sqsubseteq P,m)
,
(c,d,r_{0}\times m_{0}\times m)\in P^{\mathcal{I}}
, so that

(c,r\times m_{0}\times m\times n\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})\in(\exists P.C)^{\mathcal{I}}
(again, this holds because we are multiplying monomials).

Finally, since
\mathcal{I}\models(\exists P.C\sqsubseteq D,o)
,

(c,r\times m_{0}\times m\times n\times o\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{i}\times n_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}o_{i})\in D^{\mathcal{I}}
, as required for the GCI
\alpha
.

\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{1}

(\alpha,{m})=(B(a),{m_{1}\times\dots\times m_{k}\times m})
and the induction hypothesis applies to the axioms
(A_{i}(a),m_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq k
, and
(A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}\sqsubseteq B,m)
.
Since
\mathcal{I}\models(A_{i}(a),m_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,
it follows that
(a^{\mathcal{I}},m_{1}\times\dots\times m_{k})\in(A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{n})^{\mathcal{I}}
.
By induction hypothesis on
(A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}\sqsubseteq B,m)
, since
m_{1}\times\dots\times m_{k}
is a monomial, it follows that
(a^{\mathcal{I}},m_{1}\times\dots\times m_{k}\times m)\in B^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Hence
\mathcal{I}\models(B(a),{m_{1}\times\dots\times m_{k}\times m})
, as required for the assertion
\alpha
.

\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{2}

to
\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{5}
are easy to show in a similar way.

\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{6}

and
\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{7}
are never applied because
\mathcal{O}
is satisfiable. We show this for
\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{6}
case.
Assume for a contradiction that
(\alpha,{m})=(\bot(a),{m_{1}\times m_{2}\times m_{3}})
has been added to
\mathcal{S}_{i}
to obtain
\mathcal{S}_{i+1}
by
\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{6}
. In this case,
the induction hypothesis applies to
(R(a,b),m_{1})
and
(S(a,b),m_{2})
, while by the form of axioms that can be added by the saturation rules, either
(R\sqcap S\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
is in
\mathcal{O}
, or
(\mathsf{inv}(R)\sqcap\mathsf{inv}(S)\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
is in
\mathcal{O}
. Thus,
\mathcal{I}\models(R(a,b),m_{1})
,
\mathcal{I}\models(S(a,b),m_{2})
and
\mathcal{I}\models(R\sqcap S\sqsubseteq\bot,m_{3})
.
It must be the case that
(R\sqcap S)^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
while
(a^{\mathcal{I}},b^{\mathcal{I}},m_{1}\times m_{2})\in(R\sqcap S)^{\mathcal{I}}
, which is a contradiction. Hence
\mathsf{CR}^{A}_{6}
is never applied when
\mathcal{O}
is satisfiable.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following lemma will be useful to handle RIs in the proofs of this section.

Lemma D.2
.

If
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, then

1.

(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
;

2.

P_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P_{2}
, where
\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}
is the transitive closure of the relation
\{(S,P),(\mathsf{inv}(S),\mathsf{inv}(P))\mid S\sqsubseteq P\in\mathcal{O}\}\cup\{(R,R),(R^{-},R^{-})\mid R\in\mathsf{sig}(\mathcal{O})\cap{\sf N_{R}}\}
.

Proof.

We show by induction on the number of completion rules applied before adding
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)
to
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
that
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
and
P_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P_{2}
.

•

Base case:
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathcal{S}
. If
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathcal{O}
then
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)\in\mathcal{S}
. Otherwise,
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathcal{S}\setminus\mathcal{O}
and there is two possibilities by definition of
\mathcal{S}
: either
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)=(P\sqsubseteq P,1)
, or
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)=(\mathsf{inv}(P_{3})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{4}),m)
for some
(P_{3}\sqsubseteq P_{4},m)\in\mathcal{O}
. Since for every
R
, both
(R\sqsubseteq R,1)
and
(R^{-}\sqsubseteq R^{-},1)
are in
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathsf{inv}(\mathsf{inv}(P))=P
, it follows that in every case,
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
. Moreover, in every case,
P_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P_{2}
.

•

Assume that for every
(P\sqsubseteq Q,m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
obtained after at most
k
applications of the completion rules,
(\mathsf{inv}(P)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(Q),m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
and
P\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}Q
, and let
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
be obtained after applying
k+1
completion rules.
Since the only completion rule that adds role inclusions is
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}
,
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m)
has been added by applying
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}
to some
(P_{1}\sqsubseteq P^{\prime},m_{1})
and
(P^{\prime}\sqsubseteq P_{2},m_{2})
in
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
such that
m_{1}\times m_{2}=m
.
By induction hypothesis,
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P^{\prime}),m_{1})
and
(\mathsf{inv}(P^{\prime})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m_{2})
are in
\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, and
P_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P_{2}
.
Hence, by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}
,
(\mathsf{inv}(P_{1})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{2}),m)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, and by definition of
\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}
,
P_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{O}}P_{2}
. ∎

For point (2) of Theorem
6.4
, i.e. completeness of the completion algorithm, we will use the canonical model
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{I}_{i}
of
\mathcal{O}
(cf. Figure
3
).
The next lemmas will be crucial to handle the anonymous part of the canonical model in several proofs.

Lemma D.3
.

For all
i\geq 0
,
x,y\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
and
R
role name or inverse role, if
(x,y,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and
y\notin{\sf N_{I}}
has been introduced between
\mathcal{I}_{j-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{j}
(
j\leq i
) to satisfy an inclusion of the form
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
to some
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{j-1}}
,
then there exists
(S\sqsubseteq R,m_{S\sqsubseteq R})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
such that
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq R}=o
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
l=i-j
.

Base case:
l=0
.
The only possibility to obtain
(x,y,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
by a single application of the chase rule that introduces
y
to satisfy an inclusion of the form
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})
with some
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
is that
S=R
and
s\times m_{0}=o
. Since
(R\sqsubseteq R,1)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, we obtain the property.

Inductive step.

Assume that the property is true for every integer up to
l
and consider the case where
i-j=l+1
.
Let
x,y\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
, and assume that
(x,y,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
y\notin{\sf N_{I}}
has been introduced between
\mathcal{I}_{j-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{j}
, to satisfy
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
to
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{j-1}}
(thus adding
(x,y,s\times m_{0})\in S^{\mathcal{I}_{j}}
).
We make a case analysis on whether
(x,y,o)\in R^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
has been added by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{1}
or
\mathsf{R}_{2}
with a RI of the form:

•

(P_{p-1}\sqsubseteq R,r_{p})\in\mathcal{O}
:
It holds that
(x,y,o^{\prime\prime})\in P_{p-1}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
(with
o=o^{\prime\prime}\times r_{p}
) so by induction hypothesis (since
i-1-j=l
and the property is assumed to be true for
l
), there exists
(S\sqsubseteq P_{p-1},m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{p-1}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
such that
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{p-1}}=o^{\prime\prime}
, which implies by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}
that
(S\sqsubseteq R,m_{S\sqsubseteq R})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
with
m_{S\sqsubseteq R}=m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{p-1}}\times r_{p}
so that
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq R}=o^{\prime\prime}\times r_{p}=o
.

•

(P_{p-1}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(R),r_{p})\in\mathcal{O}
: It holds that
(x,y,o^{\prime\prime})\in\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1})^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
(with
o=o^{\prime\prime}\times r_{p}
) so by induction hypothesis there exists
(S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1}),m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1})})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
with
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1})}=o^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
(P_{p-1}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(R),r_{p})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, by Lemma
D.2
,

(\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1})\sqsubseteq R,r_{p})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
.
Hence, by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{1}
,
(S\sqsubseteq R,m_{S\sqsubseteq R})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
with
m_{S\sqsubseteq R}=m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P_{p-1})}\times r_{p}
so that
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq R}=o^{\prime\prime}\times r_{p}=o
.
∎

For a multiset
\{A_{1},\dots,A_{k}\}
of concept names, we use
\mathsf{Conj}(A_{1},\dots,A_{k})
and
\mathsf{Conj}(A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k})
to denote the conjunction obtained from
A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}
by limiting the number of times each concept name can occur to
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
, i.e. replacing
\underbrace{A\sqcap\dots\sqcap A}_{\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n\text{ times}}
by
\underbrace{A\sqcap\dots\sqcap A}_{\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})\text{ times}}
.

Lemma D.4
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and for every
j
such that
1\leq j\leq k
, assume that we are given
k_{j}\geq 0
and
k_{j}^{\prime}\geq 0
, monomials
s^{j}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
, and a set of GCIs of the form:

•

(S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}})
,
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
,

•

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}
.

Assume that all monomials are built from variables that annotate
\mathcal{O}
.

If
B\in{\sf N_{C}}
occurs
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
times in the multiset
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{B^{j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}\cup\{B^{\prime j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}\}
, then there are
n
occurrences of
B
that we can assume to correspond to
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{B^{j}_{h_{j}+1},\dots,B^{j}_{k_{j}},B^{\prime j}_{h^{\prime}_{j}+1},\dots,B^{\prime j}_{k^{\prime}_{j}}\}
for some
h_{j}\leq k_{j}
and
h^{\prime}_{j}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}_{j}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\ \Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{h_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}_{j}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}.

Proof.

Assume w.l.o.g. that for every
1\leq j\leq k
, the occurences of the concept name
B
come last in each multiset
\{B^{j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}
and
\{B^{\prime j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}\}
, that is, there exist
r_{j}\leq k_{j}
and
r^{\prime}_{j}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}
such that
\{B^{j}_{i}\mid r_{j}+1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}
and
\{B^{\prime j}_{i}\mid r^{\prime}_{j}+1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}\}
contains only
B
and there is no occurence of
B
in
\{B^{j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq r_{j}\}
and
\{B^{\prime j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq r_{j}^{\prime}\}
.

Since there are
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
occurences of the concept name
B
in total, the multiset of monomials
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i}\mid r_{j}+1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}\cup\{m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}\mid r^{\prime}_{j}+1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\}
contains
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
monomials. The total number of variables that may occur in some monomial is
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
, so among
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
monomials over
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
variables, there must be
n
monomials such that all their variables are present in the product of the remaining
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
monomials. We can assume w.l.o.g. (by re-ordering the GCIs and monomials) that they come last in each multiset
\{m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i}\mid r_{j}+1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}
and
\{m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}\mid r^{\prime}_{j}+1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\}
, that is, there exist
r_{j}+1\leq h_{j}\leq k_{j}
and
r^{\prime}_{j}+1\leq h^{\prime}_{j}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=r_{j}+1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i})\times\Pi_{i=r^{\prime}_{j}+1}^{k^{\prime}_{j}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\ \Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=r_{j}+1}^{h_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i})\times\Pi_{i=r^{\prime}_{j}+1}^{h^{\prime}_{j}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}.

The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and for every
j
such that
1\leq j\leq k
, assume that we are given
k_{j}\geq 0
, monomials
s^{j}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
, and a set of GCIs of the form:

•

(S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}})
,
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
.

Assume that all monomials are built from variables that annotate
\mathcal{O}
.

If
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
occurs
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
times in the multiset
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{A^{j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}
, then there are
n
occurrences of
A
that we can assume to correspond to
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{A^{j}_{h_{j}+1},\dots,A^{j}_{k_{j}}\}
for some
h_{j}\leq k_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i})=\ \Pi_{j=1}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{h_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i}).

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
D.4
. If
A\in{\sf N_{C}}
occurs
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})+n
times in the multiset
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{A^{j}_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{j}\}
, then there are
n
occurrences of
A
that we can assume (by re-ordering the GCIs) to correspond to
\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\{A^{j}_{h_{j}+1},\dots,A^{j}_{k_{j}}\}
for some
h_{j}\leq k_{j}
and are such that (by the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
D.4
) every variable in the corresponding monomials
m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}}\times s^{j}_{i}
occurs in the product of the remaining
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
monomials of this form with
A^{j}_{i}=A
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

For all
i\geq 0
,
x,y\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
and
A\in{\sf N_{C}}\cup\{\top\}
if
(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and
y\notin{\sf N_{I}}
has been introduced between
\mathcal{I}_{j-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{j}
(
j\leq i
) to satisfy an inclusion of the form
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
to some
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{j-1}}
,
then the following holds.

1.

There exist
k\geq 0
and
k^{\prime}\geq 0
such that:

•

(S\sqsubseteq P_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}})
and
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,

•

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}
,

•

(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq A,p)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A_{1},\dots,A_{k})=C\sqcap A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}
(i.e. there is no concept name that occurs more than
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
times in
\{C,A_{1},\dots,A_{k}\}
).

Moreover, if
A=\top
, then
k=k^{\prime}=0
and
p=1
(recall that the empty conjunction is
\top
and
(\top\sqsubseteq\top,1)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
).

2.

For every
1\leq\ell\leq k
, there exists
(x,s_{\ell})\in A_{\ell}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
.

3.

The monomials are related as follows:

•

If
k\neq 0
,
s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p=o^{\prime}
.

•

If
k=0
,

\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p=o^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
l=i-j
. In the case where
A=\top
, the property trivially holds with
k=k^{\prime}=0
for every
l\geq 0
.

Base case.

In the case where
A\neq\top
, the base case is
l=1
:
y\notin{\sf N_{I}}
has been introduced between
\mathcal{I}_{i-2}
and
\mathcal{I}_{i-1}
to satisfy
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
to
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{i-2}}
, adding
(x,y,s\times m_{0})\in S^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
. By construction of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}
, it must be the case that either (i) the chase rule has been applied between
\mathcal{I}_{i-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{i}
with
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(S).\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})
(case
\mathsf{R}_{6}
or
\mathsf{R}_{5}
), so that
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(S).\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})\in\mathcal{O}
, and
s\times m_{0}\times m_{1}=o^{\prime}
,
or (ii) the chase rule has been applied between
\mathcal{I}_{i-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{i}
with
(\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})
(case
\mathsf{R}_{3}
), so that
(\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})\in\mathcal{O}
, and
m_{1}=o^{\prime}
.
This shows the property in this case:

1.

In case (i), for
k=1
and
k^{\prime}=0
, it holds that

•

(S\sqsubseteq S,1)
and
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(S).\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
P_{1}=S
,
A_{1}=\top
,
B_{1}=A
and
m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{1}}=1
,
m_{\mathsf{inv}(P_{1}).A_{1}\sqsubseteq B_{1}}=m_{1}
),

•

(A\sqsubseteq A,1)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
B_{1}=A
and
p=1
),

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,\top)=C
.

In case (ii), for
k=0
and
k^{\prime}=1
, it holds that

•

(\top\sqsubseteq A,m_{1})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
B^{\prime}_{1}=A
and
m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}=m_{1}
)

•

(A\sqsubseteq A,1)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
B^{\prime}_{1}=A
and
p=1
),

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C)=C
.

2.

In the case
k=1
,
(x,1)\in\top^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
holds trivially (recall that
A_{1}=\top
and take
s_{1}=1
).

3.

The monomials are related as follows:

•

If
k\neq 0
, we are in case (i) and
s\times m_{0}\times 1\times 1\times m_{1}\times 1=o^{\prime}
.

•

If
k=0
, we are in case (ii) and

m_{1}\times 1=o^{\prime}
.

Inductive step.

Assume that the property is true for every integer up to
l
and consider the case where
i-j=l+1
.
Let
x,y\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}}
, and assume that
(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and
y\notin{\sf N_{I}}
has been introduced between
\mathcal{I}_{j-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{j}
, to satisfy
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
to
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{j-1}}
(thus adding
(x,y,s\times m_{0})\in S^{\mathcal{I}_{j}}
).
We make a case analysis on the last chase rule applied to add
(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
(among cases
\mathsf{R}_{3}
,
\mathsf{R}_{4}
,
\mathsf{R}_{5}
and
\mathsf{R}_{6}
).

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}_{3}}
:
\boldsymbol{(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}}
added by applying the chase rule with

\boldsymbol{(D\sqsubseteq A,m_{D\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathcal{O}}.

There are
(y,o)\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
and
o^{\prime}=m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times o
.
Since
(y,o)\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
, by induction hypothesis:

1.

There exist
k\geq 0
and
k^{\prime}\geq 0
such that:

•

(S\sqsubseteq P_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}})
and
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,

•

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}
,

•

(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq D,p^{\prime})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A_{1},\dots,A_{k})=C\sqcap A_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A_{k}
.

2.

For every
1\leq\ell\leq k
, there exists
(x,s_{\ell})\in{A_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-2}}
.

3.

The monomials are related as follows:

•

If
k\neq 0
,
s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p^{\prime}=o
.

•

If
k=0
,

\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p^{\prime}=o
.

Since
(D\sqsubseteq A,m_{D\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
and
(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq D,p^{\prime})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{2}
,

(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq A,m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times p^{\prime})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
.

We thus obtain items 1 and 2 of the property by taking
p=m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times p^{\prime}
.
To obtain item 3 of the property, we observe that the monomials are related as follows:

\displaystyle\text{If $k\neq 0$: }o^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times o

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times s\times m_{0}\times(\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p^{\prime})

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p.

If
k=0
:

o^{\prime}=m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times o=m_{D\sqsubseteq A}\times(\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p^{\prime})=\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p
.

This shows the property in this case.

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}_{4}}
:
\boldsymbol{(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}}
added by applying the chase rule with

\boldsymbol{(D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A,m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathcal{O}}.

There are
(y,o_{1})\in D_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
and
(y,o_{2})\in D_{2}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
and
o^{\prime}=m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times o_{1}\times o_{2}
.
For
j\in\{1,2\}
, since
(y,o_{j})\in D_{j}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
, by induction hypothesis:

1.

There exist
k_{j}\geq 0
and
k_{j}^{\prime}\geq 0
such that:

•

(S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}})
and
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
,

•

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}
,

•

(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq D_{j},p_{j})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A^{j}_{1},\dots,A^{j}_{k_{j}})=C\sqcap A^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A^{j}_{k_{j}}
.

2.

For every
1\leq\ell\leq k_{j}
, there exists
(x,s^{j}_{\ell})\in{A^{j}_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-2}}
.

3.

The monomials are related as follows:

•

If
k_{j}\neq 0
,
s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}\times p_{j}=o_{j}
.

•

If
k_{j}=0
,

\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}\times p_{j}=o_{j}
.

We thus have the following relationships between monomials (
\dagger
). If
k_{1}\neq 0
or
k_{2}\neq 0
:

\displaystyle o^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times o_{1}\times o_{2}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times s\times m_{0}

\displaystyle\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}(\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}\times p_{j})

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}\times m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times p_{1}\times p_{2}

\displaystyle\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}.

If
k_{1}=k_{2}=0
:

o^{\prime}=m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times o_{1}\times o_{2}=m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}p_{j}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}
.

By Lemmas
D.4
and
D.5
, we can choose
k_{1}
,
k^{\prime}_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
k^{\prime}_{2}
and the RIs and GCIs from point (1) above such that (i)
\mathsf{Conj}(\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}}))=\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}})
, i.e. there is no concept name that occurs more than
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
times in the multiset formed by all
B^{j}_{i}
’s and
B^{\prime j}_{i}
’s, (ii)
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A^{1}_{1},\dots,A^{1}_{k_{1}},A^{2}_{1},\dots,A^{2}_{k_{2}})=C\sqcap\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(A^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A^{j}_{k_{j}})
, i.e. there is no concept name that occurs more than
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
times in the multiset formed by all
A^{j}_{i}
’s, and (iii) the relationships (
\dagger
) are still true (even if it may be the case that those of point (3) are not).

Since
(D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A,m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
and
(B^{1}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{1}_{k_{1}}\sqcap B^{\prime 1}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime 1}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq D_{1},p_{1})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{2}
,

(\mathsf{Conj}(B^{1}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{1}_{k_{1}}\sqcap B^{\prime 1}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime 1}_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\sqcap D_{2})\sqsubseteq A,m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times p_{1})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
.
By applying again
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{2}
, since
\mathsf{Conj}(\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}}))=\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}})
, we obtain
(\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{2}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}})\sqsubseteq A,p)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
with
p=m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times p_{1}\times p_{2}
.

If we let
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
,
k^{\prime}=k^{\prime}_{1}+k^{\prime}_{2}
,
f(i,j)=\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{j-1}k_{\ell}+i
and
f^{\prime}(i,j)=\Sigma_{\ell=1}^{j-1}k^{\prime}_{\ell}+i
, we can rename the
P_{i}^{j}
,
A_{i}^{j}
,
B_{i}^{j}
,
{B^{\prime}_{i}}^{j}
and
s_{\ell}^{j}
by
P_{f(i,j)}
,
A_{f(i,j)}
,
B_{f(i,j)}
,
B^{\prime}_{f^{\prime}(i,j)}
and
s_{f(\ell,j)}
respectively to obtain items 1 and 2 of the property.
To obtain item 3 of the property, we observe that the monomials are related as follows:

If
k\neq 0
, i.e.
k_{1}\neq 0
or
k_{2}\neq 0
:

\displaystyle o^{\prime}=

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}\times m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times p_{1}\times p_{2}

\displaystyle\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}\times p\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).A_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}.

If
k=0
, i.e.
k_{1}=k_{2}=0
:

\displaystyle o^{\prime}=m_{D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq A}\times p_{1}\times p_{2}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{2}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}}=p\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}.

This shows the property in this case.

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}_{5}}
or
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}_{6}}
:
\boldsymbol{(y,o^{\prime})\in A^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}}
added by applying the chase rule with

\boldsymbol{(\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A,m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathcal{O}}.

There exist
(y,z,o_{yz})\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
and
(z,o_{z})\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
such that
o^{\prime}=o_{yz}\times o_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}
. We distinguish two subcases:

(i)

either
z=x
,

(ii)

or
z\neq x
, which implies that
z
has been introduced between
j^{\prime}-1
and
j^{\prime}
(
j\leq j^{\prime}\leq i
) to satisfy an inclusion of the form
(E_{0}\sqsubseteq\exists S_{z},m_{z})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
.

In case (i), it holds that

(y,x,o_{yx})\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
,
(x,o_{x})\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
and
o^{\prime}=o_{yx}\times o_{x}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}
.
Since
(y,x,o_{yx})\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
, i.e.
(x,y,o_{yx})\in\mathsf{inv}(P)^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
, and
y
has been introduced to satisfy
(C\sqsubseteq\exists S,m_{0})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
to some
(x,s)\in C^{\mathcal{I}_{j-1}}
,
then by Lemma
D.3
, there is
(S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P),m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P)})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
s.t.
s\times m_{0}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P)}=o_{yx}
.
This shows the property in case (i), indeed:

1.

If we take
k=1
and
k^{\prime}=0
, it holds that:

•

(S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P),m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P)})
and
(\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A,m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
P_{1}=\mathsf{inv}(P)
,
A_{1}=D
,
B_{1}=A
),

•

(A\sqsubseteq A,1)\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(take
B_{1}=A
and
p=1
),

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,D)=C\sqcap D
.

2.

(x,o_{x})\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
(take
A_{1}=D
and
s_{1}=o_{x}
).

3.

The monomials are related as follows (and
k\neq 0
):

\displaystyle o^{\prime}=o_{yx}\times o_{x}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}=s\times m_{0}\times o_{x}\times m_{S\sqsubseteq\mathsf{inv}(P)}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}\times 1.

We now consider case (ii):
(y,z,o_{yz})\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
,
(z,o_{z})\in D^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
,
o^{\prime}=o_{yz}\times o_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}
and
z
has been introduced between
j^{\prime}-1
and
j^{\prime}
(
j\leq j^{\prime}\leq i
) to satisfy an inclusion of the form
(E_{0}\sqsubseteq\exists S_{z},m_{z})\in\mathcal{O}
by applying the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
.
There must exist
(y,s_{0})\in E_{0}^{\mathcal{I}_{j^{\prime}-1}}
to which the chase rule in case
\mathsf{R}_{7}
or
\mathsf{R}_{8}
has been applied to satisfy
(E_{0}\sqsubseteq\exists S_{z},m_{z})
.
Hence
by Lemma
D.3
there exists
(S_{z}\sqsubseteq P,m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
such that
s_{0}\times m_{z}\times m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P}=o_{yz}
,
and by induction hypothesis, the following statements hold.

•

There exist
k\geq 0
and
k^{\prime}\geq 0
such that:

–

(S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i},m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i}})
and
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k
,

–

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}
,

–

(B_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B_{k}\sqcap B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq D,p_{z})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,

and
\mathsf{Conj}(E_{0},E_{1},\dots,E_{k})=E_{0}\sqcap E_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap E_{k}
.

Since
(\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A,m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A})\in\mathcal{O}
and
(E_{0}\sqsubseteq\exists S_{z},m_{z})\in\mathcal{O}
, it follows by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{3}
that
(E_{0}\sqcap E_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap E_{k}\sqsubseteq A,r_{z})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
(as
\mathsf{Conj}(E_{0},E_{1},\dots,E_{k})=E_{0}\sqcap E_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap E_{k}
)
with
r_{z}=m_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}\times m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p_{z}
.

•

For every
1\leq\ell\leq k
, there exists
(y,s_{\ell})\in E_{\ell}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
.

•

The monomials are related as follows:

–

If
k\neq 0
,
s_{0}\times m_{z}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k}s_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p_{z}=o_{z}
.

–

If
k=0
,

\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p_{z}=o_{z}
.

For every
0\leq j\leq k
, since
(y,s_{j})\in E_{j}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
, by induction hypothesis:

•

There exist
k_{j}\geq 0
and
k_{j}^{\prime}\geq 0
such that:

–

(S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i},m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}})
,
(\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i},m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}
,

–

(\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i},m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime j}_{i}})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,
1\leq i\leq k_{j}^{\prime}
,

–

(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq E_{j},p_{j})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
,

and
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A^{j}_{1},\dots,A^{j}_{k_{j}})=C\sqcap A^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A^{j}_{k_{j}}
.

•

For every
1\leq\ell\leq k_{j}
, there exists
(x,s^{j}_{\ell})\in{A^{j}_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{I}_{i-1}}
.

•

The monomials are related as follows:

–

If
k_{j}\neq 0
,
s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq{B^{\prime}}^{j}_{i}}\times p_{j}=s_{j}
.

–

If
k_{j}=0
,

\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq{B^{\prime}}^{j}_{i}}\times p_{j}=s_{j}
.

Combining the equalities we have, we obtain the following:
if
k\neq 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle o^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle o_{yz}\times o_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle s_{0}\times m_{z}\times m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P}\times\Pi_{j=1}^{k}s_{j}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p_{z}

\displaystyle\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=0}^{k}s_{j}\!\times\!\Pi_{i=1}^{k}(m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P_{i}}\!\times\!m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}).E_{i}\sqsubseteq B_{i}})\!\times\!\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\!\times\!p_{z}\!\times\!m_{z}\!\times\!m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P}\!\times\!m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=0}^{k}s_{j}\times r_{z}.

If
k=0
, it holds that

\displaystyle o^{\prime}=o_{yz}\times o_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}=s_{0}\times m_{z}\times m_{S_{z}\sqsubseteq P}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{i}}\times p_{z}\times m_{\exists P.D\sqsubseteq A}=s_{0}\times r_{z}.

So in both cases,
o^{\prime}=\Pi_{j=0}^{k}s_{j}\times r_{z}
.

We thus have the following relationships between monomials (
\dagger
). When
\Sigma_{j=0}^{k}k_{j}\neq 0
:

\displaystyle o^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\Pi_{j=0}^{k}s_{j}\times r_{z}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}\times\Pi_{j=0}^{k}(\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq{B^{\prime}}^{j}_{i}}\times p_{j})\times r_{z}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle s\times m_{0}

\displaystyle\times\Pi_{j=0}^{k}\Pi_{\ell=1}^{k_{j}}s^{j}_{\ell}\times\Pi_{j=0}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}}(m_{S\sqsubseteq P^{j}_{i}}\times m_{\exists\mathsf{inv}(P_{i}^{j}).A^{j}_{i}\sqsubseteq B^{j}_{i}})\times\Pi_{j=0}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq{B^{\prime}}^{j}_{i}}

\displaystyle\times\Pi_{j=0}^{k}p_{j}\times r_{z}

When
\Sigma_{j=0}^{k}k_{j}=0
:

o^{\prime}=\Pi_{j=0}^{k}s_{j}\times r_{z}=\Pi_{j=0}^{k}\Pi_{i=1}^{k_{j}^{\prime}}m_{\top\sqsubseteq{B^{\prime}}^{j}_{i}}\times p_{j}\times r_{z}
.

Again, by Lemmas
D.4
and
D.5
, we can choose
k_{j}
,
k^{\prime}_{j}
and the RIs and GCIs above such that (i)
\mathsf{Conj}(\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{k}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}}))=\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{k}(B^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{j}_{k_{j}}\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime j}_{k_{j}^{\prime}})
, i.e. there is no concept name that occurs more than
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
times in the multiset formed by all
B^{j}_{i}
’s and
B^{\prime j}_{i}
, (ii)
\mathsf{Conj}(C,A^{1}_{1},\dots,A^{1}_{k_{1}},\dots,A^{k}_{1},\dots,A^{k}_{k_{k}})=C\sqcap\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{k}(A^{j}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap A^{j}_{k_{j}})
, i.e. there is no concept name that occurs more than
\mathsf{Card}(\mathcal{O})
times in the multiset formed by all
A^{j}_{i}
’s, and (iii) the relationships (
\dagger
) are still true.

Since
(E_{0}\sqcap E_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap E_{k}\sqsubseteq A,r_{z})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
and
(B^{0}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{0}_{k_{0}}\sqcap B^{\prime 0}_{1}\sqcap\dots\sqcap B^{\prime 0}_{k_{0}^{\prime}}\sqsubseteq E_{0},p_{0})\in\mathsf{saturate}(\mathcal{O})
, by
\mathsf{CR}^{T}_{2}
,
